I just keep replaying what I should have done for my tío before he died. Like... I should have called him more often, or made the time to visit him sooner. I just end up feeling like I really failed him as family.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed any change in your weight since your uncle passed away?\n\nPatient: No, not really. My weight has stayed pretty much the same. I haven't noticed any changes or, you know, my clothes fitting differently or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you had to take any days off work due to how you've been feeling?\n\nPatient: I haven't actually taken any days off, no. I'm not as reliable with the details right now.\n\nDoctor: Have you been avoiding social situations or gatherings since your uncle's death?\n\nPatient: No, I haven't really been avoiding people. I mean, I still go to family things and see friends when they ask. I just... I might not be the life of the party right now, but I'm not hiding away or anything.\n\nDoctor: Have you been feeling like you're moving more slowly than usual?\n\nPatient: No, I don't think so. Physically, I feel like I'm moving at my normal pace. I haven't felt weighed down or sluggish or anything like that.\n\nDoctor: Have you noticed yourself pulling back or participating less in hobbies or things you normally enjoy since your uncle passed away?\n\nPatient: No, not really. I still catch the games on the weekend and do my normal stuff, so I wouldn't say I've pulled back from any of that.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt less interested in things you usually enjoy doing since your uncle passed away?\n\nPatient: No, I... I still enjoy them. Like I was just saying about my hobbies, I still like doing my normal stuff. I haven't really lost interest or stopped caring about the things I usually get a kick out of.\n\nDoctor: Have you felt like you're going through the motions when doing things you normally enjoy?\n\nPatient: No, I wouldn't say that.
